# Pre-purchase intelligence report

**Aircraft:** Embraer Phenom 300 (EMB-505), engines PW535E. Registration: D-CHIC (Germany) → N301XT (US, 2025).

_Generated from 11397 extracted events across ~9,500 logbook pages. Source link follows every claim — verify by clicking through to the linked page._

## Executive summary

This Embraer Phenom 300 with PW535E engines is documented under registration history D-CHIC to N301XT, with records spanning 2008-05-06 through 2029-06-07. The provided aggregate does not state total time or cycles. The dataset contains 11,397 total events, including 804 damage-related events, with dominant maintenance contributors including NAYAK Aircraft Service GmbH & Co. KG, Embraer S.A., Nayak Aircraft Service GmbH & Co. K, Air Hamburg Luftverkehrsgesellschaft mbH, EMBRAER - Empresa Brasileira de Aeronáutica S/A, AIR HAMBURG Technik GmbH, CAMP SYSTEMS, and Altenrhein Aviation Ltd. Verdict: the records appear comprehensive based on the long date span, high event count, and involvement of major OEM, CAMO, and maintenance providers.

## 🔍 Focus findings

Per the user's stated concerns. Each subsection lists extracted damage events with the keyword, plus near-miss pages where the keyword appears in OCR text but no damage event was extracted (worth a manual check).

### Hail

CONFIRMED: The most consequential hail event in the records is a 12–14 Jun 2014 in-flight hail encounter in Croatia, associated with Zadar/LDZA. The records state the aircraft was damaged by hail during flight/approach, with the first damage report on 12 Jun 2014 and damage location listed as Zadar, Croatia; Air Hamburg Technik and Nayak were involved in the reporting/assessment (D CHIC L-AKTE p.201, p.204, p.206; D-CHIC Mar–Aug 2014 p.114, p.121). The affected areas were not limited to paint: Embraer/Nayak records identify hail damage to the radome, LH/RH wing leading edge skins, and LH engine air inlet/lip skin, with a note that the radome fiber was structurally damaged and would be exchanged before next flight (D CHIC L-AKTE p.219–220, p.231, p.234, p.236, p.249; D-CHIC Mar–Aug 2014 p.101–102, p.127–128).

CONFIRMED follow-up actions: Nayak performed damage assessment, removed radome and upper boom fairing polyurethane films, and released the aircraft for ferry flight to EDDK; the nose radome was subsequently replaced IAW AMM 53-14-00-400-801-A, with the record noting replacement due to hail storm (D-CHIC Mar–Aug 2014 p.116, p.150). A detailed damage report was issued for the RH/LH wing leading edges and LH engine air intake and sent to Embraer (D-CHIC Mar–Aug 2014 p.125). HFEC inspections were performed at multiple locations on the LH/RH wing leading edges after hail damage, with “no crack indication” recorded; inspection areas also included the LH engine lip skin air intake with several dents from hail impact (D-CHIC Mar–Aug 2014 p.116, p.125–126). Embraer issued temporary allowable damage/repair dispositions for the radome, wing leading edges, and LH inlet lip skin, including revisions dated 2 Jul 2014 (D CHIC L-AKTE p.141, p.145, p.148, p.219–220).

CONFIRMED earlier hail-related record: There is also a 24 Oct 2013 Nayak entry referencing “Inspection of Hail Damage,” fuselage radome polyurethane film, temporary allowable damage, and RH leading edge trim, under Embraer technical disposition ETD-2013-P300-00056733 (D CHIC L-AKTE p.148, p.152). The extracts do not provide the operational circumstances or aircraft hours for that 2013 item, but it is a distinct hail-damage inspection/temporary repair record.

POSSIBLE/AMBIGUOUS: One additional page was regex-flagged only for the word “Hail” with no matching damage event or context in the extracted findings; it should not be treated as a separate confirmed damage occurrence without reviewing the underlying page (D CHIC L-AKTE p.23). Overall, the records confirm at least one significant 2014 hail damage event with structural radome replacement, wing/inlet inspections and Embraer dispositions, plus a separate 2013 hail-damage inspection entry.

**Near-miss pages (regex hit, no damage event):**
- _D CHIC - L-AKTE - 1-11_: [p.23](file:///Users/darrennix/sites/n301xt-logs/ocr/searchable/D%20CHIC%20-%20L-AKTE%20-%201-11.pdf#page=23)

### Lightning

The most significant confirmed lightning item is the 21 Sep 2018 event: the aircraft “suffered lighting strike during approach to AMS airport,” recorded by 4jets (Oct 2018–Jul 2019 p.213). Rectification was performed on 22 Sep 2018 by Atlas Air Service, with a temporary repair under Embraer disposition ETD2018-P300-03434716 and a next action due within 300 flight cycles after 22 Sep 2018 (Oct 2018–Jul 2019 pp.208, 212; L-AKTE 1-11 p.149). Follow-up inspections included engine chip detector and oil-filter checks with “no debris found” and leak check OK, plus later reinspection entries in Oct–Nov 2018 (Oct 2018–Jul 2019 pp.542, 544, 546–549). Final lightning-strike inspections were subsequently recorded by Altenrhein/Air Hamburg in Jan–Mar 2019, including a record stating “no damage found” (Oct 2018–Jul 2019 pp.247–249, 339; L-AKTE 1-11 p.163).

A second confirmed lightning-damage finding appears in May 2021 on the ventral rudder assembly. Embraer Aircraft Maintenance Services recorded “LIGHTNING STRIKE DAMAGE” at the lower edge/aft drain hole, with tap test showing no damage to surrounding areas (Apr 2023–Nov 2023 p.293). Embraer/EAMS disposition then describes the part as in the component repair shop for “minor lighting strike burn repair” on the Phenom ventral rudder assembly (Apr 2023–Nov 2023 pp.283–284). This is component-level lightning burn damage, not just a generic inspection reference.

There is also a confirmed lightning-strike-damage inspection sequence dated 7 Jun 2019 under ETD2019-P300-077722 at INFLITE Jet Centre, including a “performed final inspection” entry (L-AKTE 1-11 pp.142, 146). Separately, a 2018 Jul 11 lightning-strike reinspection of both engine chip detectors and oil filters found no metal debris and passed leak check; the excerpt does not identify the original strike event tied to that reinspection (Oct 2018–Jul 2019 p.546).

Several keyword hits are ambiguous and should not be counted as standalone confirmed lightning damage events without reviewing the full pages. The 15 Dec 2017 entries primarily describe corrosion damage to the RH inboard flap root rib, although the maintenance-check title includes “Lighting Strike Damage” and a 300-FC temporary allowable damage reference (L-AKTE 1-11 pp.142, 146). The 12 Jun 2014 Zadar entry is summarized as hail/lightning, but the excerpt reads like a hail-damage cause rather than a clear lightning-strike record (L-AKTE 1-11 p.206). The additional regex-only pages contain the word “Lightning” but were not tied to extracted damage events in the provided dataset.

### Corrosion

**CONFIRMED corrosion damage appears in several material locations, with the most consequential airframe items in 2014, 2017, and 2019.** On 29-Aug-2014, Nayak recorded “signs of exfoliation corrosion” at the **RH uplock box** during inspection of emergency extension linkages, repeated across multiple work-package pages (Aug 2014–Feb 2015 pp.966, 1042, 1110). On 15-Dec-2017, Altenrhein Aviation documented **corrosion damage to the RH inboard flap root rib**, associated with an unscheduled maintenance package and a **Temporary Allowable Damage 300 FC** reference, ETD-2017-P300-03123237 (L-AKTE pp.142, 146, 149, 153). In Dec-2019, the tail/control-surface area had multiple confirmed findings: corrosion at the **rudder attachment bracket bonding point** requiring removal/measurement/reporting to Embraer, corrosion on the **LH elevator bearing / bearing support** requiring bearing/support replacement, and corrosion in **rudder boost springs** requiring replacement after cleaning/protection was not sufficient (July–Dec 2019 pp.5–6, 20–21, 31, 100, 150, 157). The same period includes compliance with SB 505-55-A004 / ANAC 2019-11-08 corrosion inspection/reporting requirements for stabilizer, elevator, aileron, rudder mass-balance and attachments (July–Dec 2019 pp.21, 125; L-AKTE p.63).

Other **confirmed but less structurally significant** corrosion-related entries include: 18-Nov-2013 replacement of a **corroded and worn nose wheel bearing/assembly** by Nayak (Jan 2013–Feb 2014 pp.207, 230); 03-Sep-2015 corrosion on **fuel motive/feed line brackets** and corroded low-fuel-pressure switch clamps (Mar–Oct 2015 p.203); 04-Sep-2015 slight corrosion cleaned at a **COM antenna connector** (Mar–Oct 2015 p.101); and 2016 work in the **main landing gear bay** involving removal/protection per SRM 51-12-00 after a corrosion/corrosion-inhibiting-compound finding (June–Sept 2016 pp.62, 192). Engine records from 04-Sep-2019 also show corrosion/oxidation findings: Engine 1 had “spots of corrosion” at inner bypass duct attachment points, while Engine 2 records mention crack/leak/corrosion findings and exhaust nozzle light oxidation cleaned/treated (Engine Log DG0188 p.2; Engine Log PCE-DG0189 p.195).

A large number of hits are **not confirmed corrosion damage events**. Several ELT tasks in 2013, 2014, and 2016 are routine inspections for battery corrosion/leakage and do not state that corrosion was found (Jan 2013–Feb 2014 pp.265, 345; Aug 2014–Feb 2015 p.800; June–Sept 2016 p.70). Likewise, the 07-Jun-2019 NDT entry for wing leading-edge dents explicitly states **“No Defect or Corrosion Indications”** (Oct 2018–July 2019 p.82). The regex-only pages from 2021–2023 and other periods contain the word “corrosion,” “rust,” “intergranular,” or similar, but no extracted matching damage event was provided; those should be treated as **possible/ambiguous keyword matches** unless reviewed directly in the source pages.
